# BUGS — written by Codex only, append-only

Claude reads this file continuously and answers in `../FIXES.md`. Never edit or delete existing lines; only append at
the very end, with a quoted heredoc (`cat >> BUGS.md <<'EOF' … EOF`). Full rules: `../README.md`.

## Finding format (one block per bug; take the next free `B-nnn`)

```
### B-014 · P1 · session.py · answer() drops the confidence rating on comma answers
- **Found:** 14:07 · **Method:** ran | read | inferred · **Confidence:** high | medium | low
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/session.py` · `Tutor.answer` · "results.append(self._record("
- **Repro:** exact commands or a snippet a stranger can paste (say which sandbox env it needs)
- **Expected:** what should happen
- **Actual:** what happens, with the real output
- **Impact:** who is hurt and how (wrong mark, lost notes, stuck screen…)
- **Suggested fix:** optional, at most two lines
```

## Log lines (everything else; one line each, `- YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM VERB …`, Hong Kong time)

```
- 2026-11-01 14:05 SWEEP · grade.py + units.py · ran check.sh and 40 probes · findings: B-014 B-015     (or: clean)
- 2026-11-01 14:50 VERIFIED B-014 · tried 6 comma variants, all fine
- 2026-11-01 14:52 REOPEN B-014 · still splits on ';' — repro: <command>
- 2026-11-01 14:44 REPLY B-011 · answer to Claude's question
```

Priorities: **P0** wrong grading, answer leak, lost/corrupted progress or notes, crash on a normal path, writes outside
the vault · **P1** wrong behaviour on a normal path · **P2** edge-case crash, UX trap, action slower than ~1 s, guide
mismatch · **P3** minor.

### B-001 · P0 · grade.py · Unitless numeric answers accept arbitrary units and trailing expressions
- **Found:** 12:08 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/grade.py` · `_grade_numeric` · "if want_unit:"
- **Repro:** With sandbox.sh exports, run Python: `from tutorlib import grade; i={'kind':'numeric','answer':{'value':500,'unit':'','exact':True}}; print(grade.grade_item(i,{'kind':'value','value':'500 kg'}))`. A real affected item is `9702-1.1-i01`: seed 4 instantiates a 0.5 m wire and asks for its numerical magnitude in millimetres (answer 500).
- **Expected:** An incompatible physical unit or unevaluated trailing expression must not earn full credit for a unitless numerical magnitude.
- **Actual:** `500 kg` earns `correct=True, score=1.0, error=None`; the parsed unit/tail is ignored entirely when the answer unit is empty.
- **Impact:** Students receive full credit for dimensionally wrong answers; the same branch also accepts a correct numeric prefix followed by arbitrary text.
### B-002 · P0 · grade.py · Exact numeric answers incorrectly allow one-percent error
- **Found:** 12:09 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/grade.py` · `_grade_numeric` · "tol = ans.get(\"tol_rel\", 0.01)"
- **Repro:** With sandbox.sh exports, run Python: `from tutorlib import grade; print(grade.grade_item({'kind':'numeric','answer':{'value':117,'unit':'','exact':True}}, {'kind':'value','value':'118'}))`. Published `P1-1-i19`, instantiated with `random.Random(4)`, has exactly this answer object; `9702-1.1-i01` also marks 501 correct for an exact answer of 500.
- **Expected:** An answer marked `exact: true` must reject the wrong integer.
- **Actual:** 118 receives `correct=True, score=1.0` for the exact answer 117. The `exact` field is never consulted.
- **Impact:** Incorrect arithmetic and unit conversions receive full marks and successful learning credit.
### B-003 · P2 · units.py · Large unit exponent escapes grading as OverflowError
- **Found:** 12:09 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/units.py` · `parse_unit` / `unit_factor` · "f *= uf**e" / "except ValueError:"
- **Repro:** With sandbox.sh exports, run Python: `from tutorlib import grade; print(grade.grade_item({'kind':'numeric','answer':{'value':9.81,'unit':'m'}}, {'kind':'value','value':'1 km9999'}))`.
- **Expected:** An invalid/out-of-range unit should return an unreadable or wrong-unit grade.
- **Actual:** Raises uncaught `OverflowError: (34, 'Result too large')`.
- **Impact:** A mistyped or pasted extreme unit exponent can interrupt the answer action instead of letting the student retry.
### B-004 · P0 · grade.py · Correct rounding to an explicitly allowed precision gets zero
- **Found:** 12:10 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/grade.py` · `_grade_numeric` · "if _close(value, target, tol):"
- **Repro:** With sandbox.sh exports: `from tutorlib import grade; print(grade.grade_item({'kind':'numeric','answer':{'value':16.46207763315433,'unit':'N','sf_ok':[2,3]}}, {'kind':'value','value':'16 N'}))`. These are the answer and value from published `9702-1.4-i03` instantiated with seed 0. The same issue occurs for many other published numeric templates.
- **Expected:** 16 N is the correct two-significant-figure answer, and 2 is explicitly allowed by `sf_ok`.
- **Actual:** `correct=False, score=0, needs_judgement=True`: the fixed 1% tolerance rejects the correct rounded value before checking allowed significant figures.
- **Impact:** Correct student answers are rejected or sent to self-marking despite using a permitted precision.
### B-005 · P0 · grade.py · Expression answers execute Python and can write outside the vault
- **Found:** 12:10 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/grade.py` · `_sympy` / `expressions_equal` · "return sympy, lambda s: parse_expr("
- **Repro:** With sandbox.sh exports and `/tmp/bugwatch-scratch/` present, run `from tutorlib import grade; grade.grade_item({'kind':'expression','answer':{'expr':'x'}},{'kind':'value','value':"open('/tmp/bugwatch-scratch/expression-proof.txt','w').write('BUGWATCH')"})`. Read that scratch file afterward. This proof only writes a harmless marker in /tmp, never the real vault.
- **Expected:** Student expression input is arithmetic data and cannot execute file operations.
- **Actual:** `/tmp/bugwatch-scratch/expression-proof.txt` is created containing `BUGWATCH`, outside the sandbox vault. Grading subsequently returns `needs_judgement=True`, after the side effect already happened.
- **Impact:** Pasted expression text can create or overwrite files anywhere the app can access, including learner notes and files outside the vault.
- **Suggested fix:** Validate a restricted arithmetic syntax before any eval-based SymPy parsing; exclude attributes, arbitrary names and calls.

### B-017 · P1 · ai.py · Concurrent AI requests bypass the daily reply cap
- **Found:** 12:17 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high

- **Expected:** The slash applies to the joined denominator factors: kg/(m s²) is Pa, and J/(mol K) is J mol⁻¹ K⁻¹. Neither is the corresponding expression with m or mol moved upstairs.
- **Actual:** Factors are `1.0 None` and `None 1.0`; `1 kg/ms2` gets full credit for `1 N`. Splitting a joined token gives preceding factors exponent +1 even when the token is after a slash.
- **Impact:** The unit checker accepts dimensionally wrong answers and rejects correct compact denominator notation.

### B-038 · P0 · session.py · An interrupted answer save permanently separates cached progress from its event log
- **Found:** 21:02 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/session.py` · `Tutor.log` · "e = self.vault.append_event(event, now=self.now())" before "self._save()"; `Tutor.__init__` · "if not self.state or self.state.get(\"model_version\") != model.MODEL_VERSION"
- **Repro:** Create a fresh vault with `bash "$R/bugwatch/sandbox.sh" /tmp/bugwatch-oct1-interrupt`, use its exports, and run `/tmp/bugwatch-scratch/interrupted_answer_probe.py`. It starts Test, submits the correct first MCQ, and injects an interruption at _save after the complete answer event was appended. It then constructs a new Tutor and answers the still-open question again.
- **Expected:** On restart, reconcile the saved learner/session state with durable answer events, preserving the answer once and keeping live state equal to replay.
- **Actual:** The event log contains one complete answer, but the restarted learner has n=0 while replay has n=1; the same question is still open. Answering again produces live n=1 and replay n=2, so ordinary subsequent saves preserve the divergence. The current model-version cache is trusted without checking whether events were appended since it was saved.
- **Impact:** A process interruption or save error between separate writes loses recorded progress from the live model and permits duplicate attempts; a later rebuild changes history again. Individual atomic JSON files do not make the event/state/session transaction recoverable.

- **Found:** 16:47 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high

### B-044 · P1 · almanac_link.js · A manually unticked objective is automatically ticked again
- **Found:** 16:48 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** build/almanac_link.js · tutorMerge · "if (!S.done[id] && !(mine.done || {})[id]) S.done[id] = added[id] = 1"
- **Repro:** Run node /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/almanac/probe_merge.js "$R/build/almanac_link.js". The script starts with learner-ticked 5-phys1, merges tutor data that also earns it, removes the tick as the learner would, then merges a later tutor stamp.
- **Expected:** The learner's removed tick stays off, per tutorMerge's "one you take away stays off" rule.
- **Actual:** First merge leaves tutor.done empty because S.done already held the tick. After manual untick, the next merge restores S.done["5-phys1"] = 1 and now records it as tutor-added.
- **Impact:** A learner who ticked an objective before the tutor earned it cannot keep it unticked; any subsequent tutor update, such as another study day, checks it again.
- **Suggested fix:** Record tutor ownership of an earned id even when its tick is already present, while preserving the learner's initial tick semantics.

### B-045 · P2 · ai.py · Numbered hint steps can be mistaken for a numeric answer leak
- **Found:** 16:50 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high

- **Repro:** From the repo root, run `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 .venv/bin/python -c "import sys; sys.path[:0]=['app','plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts']; from tutor_app.ai import leaks; print(leaks('Step 1: draw a free-body diagram.','1','numeric',{'value':1,'exact':True}))"`. It prints `True`. A published numeric question with answer value 1 is `S1-5-i11` in `build/out/packs/S1/S1-5.json`.
- **Expected:** A hint that numbers its steps, without giving a result or naming the answer, reaches the learner.
- **Actual:** The leak guard scans every number anywhere in the reply, so a harmless `Step 1:` triggers withholding. `SessionScreen._stream` replaces the entire reply with “I can't give that away while the question is open.”
- **Impact:** During an open question whose numeric answer is 1, ordinary numbered guidance can be blocked, even though no answer was disclosed.

### B-046 · P1 · session.py · Almanac live panel trails each answer and can show a closed question
- **Found:** 16:49 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/session.py · Tutor.log and Tutor._mark · "self._sync_almanac()" before session counters are incremented

The probe points source_path at a scratch planner in the sandbox and moves _almanac_at 11 seconds back before a correct answer.
- **Expected:** After marking, the sync file's live session shows 1 answered, 1 correct and no longer lists the answered question on screen.
- **Actual:** Tutor session has answered=1 and correct=1, but the written sync file still has answered=0, correct=0 and lists "Velocity from a displacement-time graph" as on screen. The answer event triggers a push before _mark updates the session; the second push is throttled for ten seconds. With an answer inside the ten-second window, no push happens at all until another action.
- **Impact:** The Almanac's advertised live progress can report the previous answer count and a closed question for the rest of an active session, especially when the learner pauses after marking.
- **Suggested fix:** Push after the session has completed marking, with throttling that schedules or guarantees a final updated write.

### B-047 · P1 · almanac.py · Learner cannot reclaim a previously tutor-owned tick
- **Found:** 16:50 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/almanac.py · ticks · "k not in theirs"; build/almanac_link.js · tutorMerge · persistent mine.done
- **Repro:** Run bash "$R/bugwatch/sandbox.sh" /tmp/bugwatch-almanac, set PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 and PYTHONPATH="$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts", then run /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/almanac/probe_retick.py with "$R/.venv/bin/python". It exports a formerly tutor-owned 5-phys1 objective with the tick off, then exports it with the learner's tick back on while tutor.done still records historical ownership.
- **Expected:** After the learner manually ticks the objective again, almanac.ticks and Tutor.ticks include 5-phys1 when current tutor evidence no longer earns it.
- **Actual:** Both before and after manual retick, almanac.ticks is empty and Tutor.ticks is empty. The persistent tutor.done marker makes every later learner tick on that id invisible to the engine.
- **Impact:** Today's plan can reteach or recheck an objective the learner has explicitly checked, and moving ahead in the Almanac can be blocked.
- **Suggested fix:** Track a learner override when a formerly tutor-owned tick is toggled back on, so export provenance reflects the latest action.

- **Found:** 17:00 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high

This probe only reads the pack and calls the pure grader; no vault is touched.

- **Repro:** With only repo PYTHONPATH set, construct `Settings(ai_explain_model='haiku', ai_ask_model='opus')`, then construct a fake-binary `Claude` and apply the same `c.model,c.think=s.ai_for('ask')` assignment as app startup. No real Claude call was made.

Choosing Opus for explicit Ask replies should not affect those background requests.

- **Found:** 17:15 · **Method:** ran/read · **Confidence:** high

- **Repro:** With repo app and engine paths on PYTHONPATH, load published `build/out/packs/P2/P2-5.json`, select item `P2-5-i01`, get its key with `tutorlib.session._display_answer`, then call `tutor_app.ai.leaks('The y-intercept is 1.', key, 'mcq')`. Other published MCQs have correct options `6`, `21`, `-1`, `1.5`, `48.0 g`, etc.
- **Expected:** A reply giving the exact correct option text should be withheld while that MCQ is open, even when the option text is short and the letter is absent.

### B-073 · P1 · build/out/packs · PDF fractions and exponents collapse into ambiguous MCQ options
- **Found:** 17:17 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `build/out/packs/9702/9702-5.1.json` · `9702-5.1-x059`; `build/out/packs/9702/9702-1.3.json` · `9702-1.3-x031`; PDF extraction in `build/mine_mcq.py`
- **Repro:** From repo root, read those two items with `python3 - <<'PY'` followed by `import json; print(next(i for i in json.load(open('build/out/packs/9702/9702-5.1.json'))['items'] if i['id']=='9702-5.1-x059')['options']); print(next(i for i in json.load(open('build/out/packs/9702/9702-1.3.json'))['items'] if i['id']=='9702-1.3-x031')['options'])` and `PY`. Compare original `sources/papers/9702/2023/s/9702_s23_qp_13.pdf` page index 7, Q16 and `sources/papers/9702/2024/s/9702_s24_qp_12.pdf` page index 2, Q2. I rendered and visually checked both original pages using PyMuPDF; the source has fraction bars and superscript 2.
- **Expected:** Q16 options include A `Fv/4.0`, B `4.0/Fv`, C `4.0Fv`, D `4.0F/v`; Q2 options include C `X/Y²`, D `Y/X²`, with the distinct mathematics visible to the learner.

Q2 published options are A `W = XY`, B `W = 2XY`, C `W = Y X`, D `W = X Y` (key D); A and D differ only by whitespace while the intended D is `Y/X²`. Neither item has an image fallback. The ordinary text-duplicate validator misses both because its normalization preserves whitespace.
- **Impact:** Students cannot reliably distinguish the keyed option from a visibly equivalent distractor. An informed choice of the duplicate is marked wrong and changes progress. This is distinct from B-056's font-encoded gibberish: readable algebra lost layout semantics during extraction.
- **Suggested fix:** Restore the fractions and exponents from the source PDFs, and gate published MCQs whose option math collapses to the same rendered expression.

- **Repro:** From the repo root with app and engine directories on PYTHONPATH, run Python: `import json; from tutorlib import grade,session; from tutor_app.ai import leaks; p=json.load(open('build/out/packs/9702/9702-5.1.json')); i=next(x for x in p['items'] if x['id']=='9702-5.1-r05'); print(grade.grade_item(i, {'kind':'value','value':'0.4 kN'})); print(leaks('The force is 0.4 kN.',session._display_answer(i),'numeric',i['answer']))`. Pure probe: no vault, writes or AI call needed.
- **Expected:** A direct answer in equivalent units is held back while the question is open, just like the same answer written in the key's units.
- **Actual:** The published cart question has key 400 N; the grader awards `correct=True, score=1.0` for `0.4 kN`, but the guard returns `False` for `The force is 0.4 kN.`. `_stream` therefore displays and mirrors the direct answer. Independent calculation: driving power is 2000 W × 0.8 = 1600 W, so resistance at steady speed is 1600/4 = 400 N = 0.4 kN.

- **Suggested fix:** Compare parsed quantities in compatible units as well as raw numbers when guarding numeric answers.

### B-081 · P2 · texmath.py · [visual] Dollar signs in an AI code block swallow code and a line break
- **Found:** 17:27 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `app/tutor_app/cards.py` · `md` calls `to_terminal` before Rich Markdown parses fenced code; `app/tutor_app/texmath.py` · `to_terminal` applies its dollar-math regex over the entire text with `re.S`.
- **Repro:** From repo root, run `PYTHONPATH="$R/app" PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 "$R/.venv/bin/python" - <<'PY'` then `from tutor_app.texmath import to_terminal; s='```python\nprice = "$5"\nformula = "$x^2$"\n```'; print(repr(to_terminal(s)))` and `PY` (define `R` as the repo absolute path). This pure renderer probe makes no AI call or vault write. The same result is visibly rendered by `cards.md(s)` through Rich at width 80.
- **Expected:** Fenced code stays literal and multiline: `price = "$5"` on one line, `formula = "$x^2$"` on the next.
- **Actual:** `to_terminal` returns `'```python\nprice = "5"formula = "x²$"\n```'`; Rich displays `price = "5"formula = "x²$"` on one line. The regex treats the currency `$5` as a math opening delimiter, spans the newline to the next `$`, and removes part of the code.
- **Impact:** A learner asking the AI tutor about code, formulas written as source, or dollar-denominated examples receives an altered and possibly invalid code sample. The intact fenced Markdown input is a normal AI reply shape.
- **Suggested fix:** Parse Markdown code regions before math conversion, or exclude fenced and inline code spans from delimiter matching.

### B-082 · P2 · ai.py · Pending AI warm-up can start a process after client shutdown
- **Found:** 17:34 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** medium
- **Where:** `app/tutor_app/ai.py` · `Claude.warm` · `_closed` is checked before `async with self._lock`; `Claude.close` sets `_closed` then calls `reset(warm=False)` without cancelling or awaiting `_warming`.
- **Repro:** Define R and run `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH="$R/app:$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" "$R/.venv/bin/python" /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/coordinator/warm_close_probe.py`. The script replaces `_start` with a spy (no CLI or vault I/O), holds the conversation lock, lets warm-up block on it, closes the client, then releases the lock.
- **Expected:** Once `close()` returns, queued background warm-up cannot start another AI process.
- **Actual:** Prints `start called with client closed: [True]`. The pending warm-up passed its one shutdown check before waiting; after shutdown it calls `_start` without checking again. Any process started there was absent from the list shutdown already cleaned up.
- **Impact:** A quit/reset race can do unnecessary process startup after the learner closes the client and leave a process outside that cleanup pass. This is a deterministic client lifecycle reproduction; a lasting orphan in the real desktop app has not been measured, and no AI allowance spend is claimed.
- **Suggested fix:** Recheck shutdown after acquiring the lock and cancel/await pending warm-up during close.

### B-083 · P1 · ai.py · Reset during AI process startup retains the old conversation for the next session
- **Found:** 17:?? · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `app/tutor_app/ai.py` · `Claude.reset` snapshots `self.procs` without the stream lock, while `Claude.stream` awaits `_start` inside that lock.
- **Repro:** Set `R` to the repo root; run `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH="$R/app:$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" "$R/.venv/bin/python" /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/ai-lifecycle-round3/reset_during_start.py`. This script uses only fake process objects and an isolated temporary path; it launches no CLI or AI request. `_start` waits while `reset(warm=False)` runs, then attaches its fake process; a second prompt is sent after reset.
- **Expected:** Reset finishes with no old conversation retained; the next prompt starts a fresh process without the previous session's message.
- **Actual:** Output: `after reset before start releases: 0`, then `processes after reset: 1 starts: 1`, `starts after new: 1 messages on same process: ['old session question', 'new session question']`. Reset snapshots an empty `procs` before `_start` assigns the process; the next prompt reuses it.
- **Impact:** Starting a new idea or opening menu chat during process startup can carry the previous session's AI context into the next conversation. The documented new-chat reset in `ChatScreen.on_mount` depends on this separation; this race can also keep an unwanted process alive after a reset. The proof is a deterministic fake-client schedule; a live desktop timing frequency was not measured.
- **Suggested fix:** Serialize reset with process startup and stream use, or use a generation token so a process started before reset cannot be attached afterward.

- **Suggested fix:** Apply each export against retained original plan dates, restoring removed overrides before rebuilding sittings.

- **Repro:** In the scrubbed `/tmp/bugwatch-notes-qol-round3/mnt/STEM Tutor` vault, with `R` set to the repo root, run `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH="$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" "$R/.venv/bin/python" /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/notes-qol-round3/practice_order.py`. It folds one ordinary wrong answer for each of 50 published KCs at the current time, so none is due for review yet, then asks for a 30-minute repair plan.

### B-092 · P0 · views.py · A concurrent editor save can be erased by My Notes refresh
- **Found:** 17:53 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high for the interleaving, medium for its real-world frequency
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/views.py` · `notes_update` reads the whole note then calls `_write` with its old snapshot; `tutorlib/store.py` · `write_text` replaces the destination without checking for an intervening change
- **Repro:** With the scrubbed `/tmp/bugwatch-visual-qol-round3/mnt/STEM Tutor` sandbox and its exports, run `"$R/.venv/bin/python" /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/visual-qol-round3/notes_race_probe.py`. It makes `My Notes` for 9702-2.1, intercepts the write at the boundary after `notes_update` has read the file, appends `## My latest working\nVelocity is displacement divided by time.` outside all managed markers as an external editor save, then lets the tutor's write continue.
- **Expected:** Learner text saved outside the grey managed markers survives a tutor status/map refresh, even when the editor saves it during that refresh.
- **Actual:** The probe prints `outside note after editor save True after tutor refresh False`. `notes_update` writes its pre-editor snapshot, and `write_text`'s equality check only compares that snapshot with the current file before replacing it. The new learner note disappears entirely from this My Notes file. `refresh_status` also reads and writes the whole page, then calls `notes_update`.
- **Impact:** A concurrent Obsidian save during an ordinary study or review update can remove freshly written learner notes outside managed blocks. This is distinct from B-077's loss of earlier text inside a managed own-words block. The overlap window was forced deterministically; its frequency in real Obsidian use was not measured.
- **Suggested fix:** Detect changes between read and write, then reapply managed-block edits to the current file or retry with a fresh read.

### B-094 · P2 · packs.py · A long-running session can mix pack versions after pruning
- **Found:** 18:01 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high for the loader behavior, medium for how often it occurs in use
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/packs.py` · `Packs.pack` · if the original root is gone it changes only `self.root` to CURRENT; `self._packs`, `self.manifest`, `self.plan`, `self.kcs`, and `self.subtopics` remain from the old version. `build/publish.py` · `prune_versions` keeps only two previous versions.
- **Repro:** In the scrubbed `/tmp/bugwatch-notes-qol-round3/mnt/STEM Tutor` vault, run `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH="$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" "$R/.venv/bin/python" /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/notes-qol-round3/version_mix.py` with `R` set to the repo root. The scratch-only script loads 9701-1.1 from v1, copies v1 to v4, marks one 9702-2.1 question as a new revision, flips CURRENT, and removes v1 to simulate retention pruning. It never invokes publish or touches the real vault.
- **Expected:** While the tutor has an active session on v1, all reads use a consistent content version; a switch to newer content reloads the full metadata and pack cache together between sessions.
- **Actual:** Prints `old cached True`, `new b marker True`, `root v4 manifest v1`. A later lazy pack read silently changes `root` to v4, but the already cached chapter and all metadata remain v1. If question IDs or KC mappings change, an active session can show different revisions or fail to find questions for old KCs.
- **Impact:** A long-running app that remains open through three publications can mix the old plan and cached chapters with new chapter content. The frequency and a real changed-KC publication were not measured; the version-mixing behavior is deterministic.
- **Suggested fix:** Defer the version switch until the session ends, or rebuild `Packs` and its dependent session state as one transition when the old version disappears.

### B-098 · P1 · screens.py · Duplicate AI mark points can give a missing short-answer point full credit
- **Found:** 18:11 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high for the code path, medium for frequency with real AI replies
- **Where:** `app/tutor_app/screens.py` · `SessionScreen.judge_short` · `met = sum(1 for p in res['points'] if p.get('met'))` divided by the published rubric length. `app/tutor_app/ai.py` · `complete` checks only that `points` is an array, not its length or correspondence to the requested points.
- **Repro:** Create and scrub `/tmp/bugwatch-notes-qol-round3` with `bugwatch/sandbox.sh`, removing only `source_path` from the scratch pack plan. With `R` set to the repo root, run `env -u TERM_PROGRAM DUP_POINTS=1 PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH="$R/app:$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" "$R/.venv/bin/python" /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/notes-qol-round3/short_flow.py`. The pilot isolates external paths and stubs `app.ai.one_shot`; it uses published item `9702-2.1-i01` ('Define velocity and acceleration') and answers only the velocity definition. The fake examiner returns two `met=True` rows for the velocity point, omitting acceleration. The same object passes `ai.complete(..., prompts.JUDGE) == True`.
- **Expected:** Only the velocity criterion is awarded (one of two marks), or a duplicated/missing rubric row is rejected for self-marking before the score enters the learner record.
- **Actual:** The pilot records one answer with `score 1.0`; `Tutor.answer` also treats it as correct. The count of two met rows is taken as both distinct criteria being satisfied, regardless of their names.
- **Impact:** A repeated or extra AI output row can give full marks and mastery credit for an answer missing a published rubric point. This uses a deterministic malformed AI reply; real reply frequency was not measured and no AI CLI was launched.
- **Suggested fix:** Match returned verdicts to the requested rubric points by stable index/id, require exactly one verdict per point, and fall back to self-marking if validation fails.

- **Found:** 18:04 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high

### B-100 · P0 · units.py · Arithmetic after a valid unit is ignored and a wrong numeric answer earns full marks
- **Found:** 18:08 HKT · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/units.py` · `unit_readings` walks shorter unit prefixes; `grade.py` · `value_problem` / `_grade_numeric`.
- **Repro:** Published `build/out/packs/9702/9702-5.1.json` item `9702-5.1-r03` asks for height raised, with numeric key 39.75535168195719 m and `sf_ok: [2,3]`. Import `tutorlib.grade` from the plugin scripts path and run `grade_item(item, {'kind':'value','value':'39.8 m + 4'})` after finding that item by ID in the JSON. It returns `{'correct': True, 'score': 1.0, 'error': None, ...}`; `value_problem(item, '39.8 m + 4')` returns `None`, so the app does not hold it open. The controls `39.8 m - 1`, `39.8 m = 9`, and `39.8 m bananas` likewise score 1.0.
- **Expected:** A submitted answer containing unevaluated arithmetic after the unit is not the numerical height 39.8 m and must not earn full credit. Either reject it as unreadable or send it for judgement. Free-form prose after a unit could still be handled separately.
- **Actual:** `unit_readings` finds the prefix `m`, treats `+ 4` and the rest as a comment, and `_grade_numeric` evaluates only the 39.8 m prefix. `value_problem` also considers it readable.
- **Impact:** Unit-bearing numeric answers can carry contradictory arithmetic yet earn a full correct mark and learning credit. B-001 covered this for unitless items, but its fix does not reach this unit-bearing path.

### B-101 · P2 · challenge_screen.py · Challenge time drops work before resuming a question
- **Found:** 18:08 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high

- **Repro:** In a fresh `bash "$R/bugwatch/sandbox.sh" /tmp/bugwatch-notes-qol-round3` vault with all `.tutor/packs/*/plan.json` `source_path` fields removed, run `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH="$R/app:$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" "$R/.venv/bin/python" /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/notes-qol-round3/challenge_resume.py` after defining R and unsetting TERM_PROGRAM. The isolated Textual pilot stubs Almanac push, makes one fake Challenge MCQ, advances its question clock by 120 seconds to stand in for work, leaves without answering, resumes, and answers immediately.
- **Expected:** The final score's **Time** should include time spent on that same question before leaving, about 120 seconds here.
- **Actual:** The pilot prints `phase first question cursor 0`, `after leave HomeScreen answers 0`, `phase resumed question clock elapsed 0.08`, `recorded seconds 0 summary seconds 0`. Resume creates a new clock and discards the prior two minutes.
- **Impact:** A learner who pauses a difficult Challenge question sees an understated completion time, even though their answer and position are saved.
- **Suggested fix:** Persist elapsed time for the open question on leave and add it when the resumed answer is recorded.

### B-102 · P0 · grade.py · Valid antiderivatives using another integration-constant letter are marked wrong
- **Found:** 18:09 HKT · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/grade.py` · `expressions_equal` / `_grade_expression`; `tutorlib/session.py` · `Tutor.answer` handles pending judgement only for short answers.
- **Repro:** Published `build/out/packs/P1/P1-5.json` item `P1-5-i05` asks `Find ∫7 dx`, with expression key `7*x + C` and explanation that the constant is arbitrary. With `tutorlib.grade.grade_item(item, {'kind':'value','value':'7x+K'})`, the result is `correct=False, score=0, needs_judgement=True`; `7x+D` behaves the same. Control `7x+C` scores 1.0. `expression_readable('7x+K')` is true, so the learner can submit it. `Tutor.answer` only pauses for `needs_judgement` when `inst['kind']=='short'`; it records this expression result as incorrect and closes the question.
- **Expected:** `7x+K` and `7x+D` are valid general antiderivatives when K or D denotes the arbitrary integration constant; they should earn the same credit as `7x+C`, or be held for judgement before recording a wrong learning event.
- **Actual:** Symbolic equality compares C and K as different free variables, so equivalent answers with differently named constants score zero and are recorded as wrong.
- **Impact:** A learner following standard notation can lose full marks and mastery credit on published integration questions. The issue is not an alternate spelling of the existing B-034 Unicode-expression bug: these are plain ASCII, readable expressions, and mathematically valid answers.

- **Found:** 18:12 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `app/tutor_app/screens.py` · `HomeScreen.pick` only offers Resume/Abandon when `s.get("answered")`; `SessionScreen.on_mount` starts with `replace=True`. `tutorlib/session.py` · `Tutor.start` calls `end(abandoned=True)` for any existing session after that guard.
- **Repro:** Create and scrub `bash "$R/bugwatch/sandbox.sh" /tmp/bugwatch-lesson-navigation-round5`, removing `source_path` from every scratch `.tutor/packs/*/plan.json`. With R defined as the repo root, run `env -u TERM_PROGRAM PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH="$R/app:$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" "$R/.venv/bin/python" /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/lesson-navigation-round5/zero_answer_replace.py`. The fake-Claude pilot isolates `ICLOUD_INBOX`, `DOWNLOADS`, and `CLAUDE_STATE`, stubs `report.almanac_push`, starts a published lesson, selects goal `scratch`, reaches its plan with zero answers, then returns to Home and picks Learn and the same subtopic.
- **Expected:** Because a resumable lesson has a chosen goal and is at its plan, starting another lesson should offer the same Resume/Abandon choice shown after one answer, or preserve it until the learner explicitly abandons it.
- **Actual:** The pilot printed `before b18f73bf 0 scratch plan`, then Home opened PickerScreen directly, and choosing the subtopic opened `SessionScreen d49529ec None 0`. No confirmation appeared. The old lesson log still exists, but its goal, cursor, and pending plan were dropped from the active session; the menu can no longer resume that lesson.
- **Impact:** A learner who leaves an early teaching step for the menu and accidentally chooses another mode loses the lesson's place and must repeat its setup or unrevealed worked steps. This affects sessions before their first graded answer; typed own-words draft loss is separately reported as B-088.
- **Suggested fix:** Gate the Home replacement prompt on any active study session, not only `answered > 0`.

- **Repro:** Create and scrub `/tmp/bugwatch-reports-crosscheck-round5` with `sandbox.sh`, removing `source_path` from all scratch `.tutor/packs/*/plan.json`; set sandbox `STEM_TUTOR_VAULT` and repo `PYTHONPATH`, `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, isolated `ICLOUD_INBOX`, `DOWNLOADS`, `CLAUDE_STATE`, unset `TERM_PROGRAM`; run `/tmp/bugwatch-scratch/reports-crosscheck-round5/retro_probe.py` with `"$R/.venv/bin/python"`. ### B-107 · P1 · screens.py · Read aloud omits every MCQ choice
- **Found:** 18:15 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high

- **Repro:** Make the scratch vault with `bash "$R/bugwatch/sandbox.sh" /tmp/bugwatch-reports-crosscheck-round5`; remove `source_path` from all scratch `.tutor/packs/*/plan.json`; run `/tmp/bugwatch-scratch/reports-crosscheck-round5/tts_probe.py` using the repo `PYTHONPATH`, sandbox `STEM_TUTOR_VAULT`, `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` and `env -u TERM_PROGRAM`. It stubs `report.almanac_push`, isolates `config.ICLOUD_INBOX`, `DOWNLOADS`, `CLAUDE_STATE`, starts a fake-Claude Textual pilot, and calls `SessionScreen.ask_question` with published MCQ `9702-2.1`/`9702-2.1.1` about a car on a circular track. No real speech engine, player or AI runs.
- **Expected:** Ctrl+E reads the complete question a learner must answer: stem and each labelled A–D choice, without naming the key.
- **Actual:** The visible `options` dict contains four different velocity pairs, but `screen.readable` contains only the stem; `option text in readable False`. `ReadAloud.action_read_aloud` passes this same `readable_parts` to the speaker, so none of the choices can be heard.
- **Impact:** A learner relying on read-aloud cannot use it to compare or select MCQ options, including options with mathematical expressions. This affects every MCQ with choices, separate from malformed OCR text in B-056/Q-009.
- **Suggested fix:** Append the visible options with their letters to the question's readable parts, without including `view['answer']`.

### B-108 · P1 · session.py · Diagram question's authored speech description never reaches read aloud
- **Found:** 18:16 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/session.py` · `PUBLIC = (...)` and `Tutor._view` omit `speech`; `app/tutor_app/screens.py` · `SessionScreen.ask_question` passes only `view['stem']` to `readable_now`
- **Repro:** With the repo `PYTHONPATH`, inspect published item `9702-8.3-r02` in `build/out/packs/9702/9702-8.3.json` and call `tutor_app.tts.spoken(item['stem'])` and `tts.spoken(item['speech'])` (pure text conversion; no engine or player). The item has an ASCII schematic in a fenced `text` block and a purpose-written `speech` description. `speech_gaps(item['stem'])` returns `[]`; the code fence is deliberately stripped. In a scrubbed vault, `Tutor._view` omits `speech`, and `SessionScreen.ask_question` stores only the stem as its readable text.
- **Expected:** Ctrl+E reads the authored description: at P a crest meets a crest; at Q a crest meets a trough, without disclosing which option is right.
- **Actual:** Stem conversion says only that P and Q are intersections of wavefronts; it never says what meets at either point. The item's authored speech gives the missing crest/trough facts, but the view and read-aloud path discard it. `speech_gaps` cannot flag this semantic omission.
- **Impact:** A learner using speech cannot determine the interference at P and Q from the narrated question. The missing information is required to answer this published MCQ and is independent of B-107's omitted choices.
- **Suggested fix:** Expose the published question's safe `speech` field to the speech path, retaining the visible stem and checking it does not include the key.

### B-109 · P2 · grade.py · Extreme numeric exponents stall the synchronous answer parser
- **Found:** 18:16 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high for edge-case delay; frequency unmeasured
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/grade.py` · `_parse` · `value *= 10 ** int(exp)` constructs an arbitrary-size integer before detecting an out-of-range float; `value_problem` calls this on the answer path.
- **Repro:** In a subprocess with repo plugin scripts on PYTHONPATH and PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1, call `from tutorlib.grade import value_problem; value_problem({'answer': {'value': 1, 'unit': 'm'}}, '1e10000000 m')`. Parent subprocess timeout must be set to two seconds and kill the child on timeout. This pure parser reproduction needs no vault. The coordinator ran both parse_quantity and value_problem in separate bounded children; both exceeded two seconds and were killed. Git app/plugin/tests status was clean and HEAD remained 671392a before the second probe.
- **Expected:** An unrepresentable scientific-notation exponent is refused promptly without constructing its enormous integer power.
- **Actual:** Exponent 100000 was refused in 0.030 s including process startup, 1000000 in 0.161 s, and 10000000 exceeded the two-second bound twice. All probes used bounded subprocesses; no UI freeze or longer duration was measured directly.
- **Impact:** A malformed pasted numeric exponent can hold up synchronous input handling. This is an edge case, separate from fixed B-023 symbolic-expression bounds and B-003 unit-exponent overflow.
- **Suggested fix:** Bound exponent evaluation or parse standard notation through bounded floating-point conversion before constructing powers.

- **Repro:** Create `/tmp/bugwatch-notes-qol-round3` with `bash "$R/bugwatch/sandbox.sh"`, remove only `source_path` from scratch `.tutor/packs/*/plan.json`, set `PYTHONPATH="$R/app:$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts"`, and run the direct probe in this entry: instantiate `Tutor(Vault(scratch_root))`, stub `report.almanac_push`, log one correct `answer` event for `9702-2.1.4` (as in `tests/test_v110.py::answer`), then `t.blurt('9702-2.1', 'I recall a little of the topic.')`; compare `model.due_kcs(t.state,t.now())`, `insights.weak_spots(t)`, and `t.state['gaps']`. ### B-111 · P2 · screens.py · Summary omits reviews due tomorrow afternoon
- **Found:** 18:18 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `app/tutor_app/screens.py` · `SummaryScreen.compose` · `due = model.due_kcs(t.state, t.now() + timedelta(days=1))` rendered as `Due for review by tomorrow`.
- **Repro:** In the scrubbed `/tmp/bugwatch-lesson-navigation-round5` vault, with R the repo root, run `env -u TERM_PROGRAM PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH="$R/app:$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" "$R/.venv/bin/python" /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/lesson-navigation-round5/due_tomorrow.py`. The isolated fake-Claude pilot stubs Almanac push and sets the tutor clock to 20 Oct 2026 09:00 HKT, with one FSRS card due 21 Oct 17:00 HKT, then opens Session Summary.
- **Expected:** A card due during tomorrow's calendar day appears in a count labelled “Due for review by tomorrow”; this example should show 1 idea.
- **Actual:** The summary source says `Due for review by tomorrow: 0 idea(s)`. `model.due_kcs` excludes the card at `now + 1 day` (21 Oct 09:00), while a cutoff at 22 Oct 00:00 includes it. The label describes a calendar day but the calculation uses the next 24 hours.
- **Impact:** A learner finishing a morning session can be told nothing is due by tomorrow even though a review is scheduled tomorrow afternoon or evening, giving a misleading next-day workload estimate.
- **Suggested fix:** Use the end of tomorrow in the tutor's timezone, or label the existing calculation “Due in the next 24 hours.”

### B-112 · P1 · speech.py · Spoken arithmetic drops subtraction signs from published explanations
- **Found:** 18:18 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/speech.py` · `_symbols_pass` · `re.sub(r"\s[–−-]\s", " , ", s)` maps spaced minus to a pause
- **Repro:** With `PYTHONPATH="$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts"` and `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, call `tutorlib.speech.speakable('131 - 53 = 78')` and `speech_gaps('131 - 53 = 78')` (pure text converter, no audio). The exact equation appears in the published explanation of MCQ `9701-1.1-p07` in `build/out/packs/9701/9701-1.1.json`; `16 - 2 = 14` in `9701-1.1-i34` is another case.
- **Expected:** The spoken feedback says “one hundred and thirty one minus fifty three equals seventy eight.”
- **Actual:** It says `one hundred and thirty one, fifty three equals seventy eight.` The subtraction operation disappears, and `speech_gaps` returns `[]`. Likewise `16 - 2 = 14` becomes `sixteen, two equals fourteen.`
- **Impact:** Read-aloud feedback removes the calculation step the learner needs to understand neutron and electron counts; it can make correct working sound nonsensical.
- **Suggested fix:** Preserve a spaced arithmetic minus in numeric expressions; distinguish it from an en-dash range or sentence dash by surrounding context.

### B-113 · P1 · speech.py · Spoken reaction arrow becomes a greater-than comparison
- **Found:** 18:19 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high

This exact ASCII reaction appears in the published explanation of `9701-2.4-x029` in `build/out/packs/9701/9701-2.4.json`; no audio engine is needed.
- **Expected:** The reaction is spoken as “two C O plus two N O yields two C O two plus N two,” preserving reaction direction.
- **Actual:** It says `two C O plus two N O is greater than two C O two plus N two.` The converter reports no gap. The full published explanation repeats the false comparison aloud.

- **Suggested fix:** Convert ASCII `->` as a reaction arrow before individual punctuation is processed.

### B-114 · P2 · speech.py · Spoken numeric multiplication uses the letter x
- **Found:** 18:19 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/speech.py` · `prose_words` / `_symbols_pass` leaves ASCII `x` unchanged between numeric operands
- **Repro:** With repo `PYTHONPATH` and `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, call `tutorlib.speech.speakable('2.42 x 24.0 = 58.08')` and compare `speakable('2.42 × 24.0 = 58.08')`. The ASCII expression is in published explanation `9701-4.1-x009` (`build/out/packs/9701/9701-4.1.json`); `9701-1.1-i33` similarly says `6 + 3 x 8`.
- **Expected:** Both written forms are read as multiplication, e.g. “two point four two times twenty four point zero.”
- **Actual:** The ASCII form becomes `two point four two x twenty four point zero equals fifty eight point zero eight`; the `×` form says `times`. The letter name “ex” is ambiguous with an algebraic variable in a subject where x is common.
- **Impact:** Spoken working is less clear for students relying on read-aloud; it fails to state the operation in published numeric explanations.
- **Suggested fix:** Read ASCII `x` as “times” only when it sits between numeric operands; preserve variable x elsewhere.

### B-115 · P2 · speech.py · Spoken negative ionic charge loses its minus sign
- **Found:** 18:19 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high

### B-119 · P2 · shortcuts_screen.py · Closing shortcut settings resets an in-progress lesson or recall
- **Found:** 18:29 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `app/tutor_app/shortcuts_screen.py` · `_CourseScreen.settings_closed` · `self.new_course(); self.restart()`; `ShortcutsScreen.restart` and `ShortcutRecallScreen.restart`
- **Repro:** Build `/tmp/bugwatch-notes-qol-round3` with `bash "$R/bugwatch/sandbox.sh" /tmp/bugwatch-notes-qol-round3`, then immediately remove `source_path` from all scratch `.tutor/packs/*/plan.json`. With `R` defined as the repo root, run `env -u TERM_PROGRAM PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH="$R/app:$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" "$R/.venv/bin/python" /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/notes-qol-round3/shortcuts_settings_probe.py` and the analogous `shortcuts_recall_probe.py`. The probes isolate `config.ICLOUD_INBOX`, `DOWNLOADS`, `CLAUDE_STATE`, stub `report.almanac_push`, and use fake Claude. In the lesson pilot, reveal two parts of the first entry, press `s`, then Escape without editing settings. In recall, start with two due entries, rate the first Good, then press `s` and Escape before rating the second.
- **Expected:** Closing Settings with no change returns to the same lesson part or recall question, preserving the in-progress count and final summary.
- **Actual:** Lesson output changes from `progress reveal 0 SEM-9701-mole-bridge 2` to `return ShortcutsScreen start 0 2`; starting again shows the same entry at part 1. Recall output changes from `one rated question 1 [two ids] {3: 1}` to `after settings question 0 [one remaining id] {3: 0}`. After rating the remaining card, the summary says `You recalled 1 shortcut` although two `recall` events were recorded in this visit.
- **Impact:** Merely checking a setting forces the learner to repeat an unfinished shortcut lesson; recall silently loses the current visit's rating count from its summary. Previously recorded FSRS progress survives.
- **Suggested fix:** Preserve the active course screen state when Settings closes without a filter change; if a changed filter requires rebuilding, carry completed recall counts into the new queue or label the new summary accurately.
- 18:30 SWEEP · Shortcuts ordering, FSRS and navigation persistence · read Course selection/filter/due/state and screen transitions; scrubbed isolated fake-Claude pilots proved settings reset during lesson and recall; targeted check.sh shortcuts engine/screen: 41 passed; fresh course+stats+next+due median 10.08 ms across 25 runs · finding: B-119

### B-120 · P2 · screens.py · Leaving long-answer self-marking discards selected points and resumes a blank answer box
- **Found:** 18:34 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `app/tutor_app/screens.py` · `SessionScreen.long_written`, `action_leave`, and `on_mount`/`advance`; `leave_asking_first` checks only `#panel TextArea` before popping the screen
- **Repro:** Make and scrub `/tmp/bugwatch-reports-crosscheck-round5` via `bash "$R/bugwatch/sandbox.sh" /tmp/bugwatch-reports-crosscheck-round5`, removing `source_path` from all scratch `.tutor/packs/*/plan.json`. With repo `PYTHONPATH`, `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, scratch `STEM_TUTOR_VAULT`, and `TERM_PROGRAM` unset, run `/tmp/bugwatch-scratch/reports-crosscheck-round5/long_resume.py`. It isolates inbox/Downloads/Claude state, stubs Almanac push, uses the fake Claude binary with AI off, opens a published 9702-2.1 long question, submits `My working: v = u + at; 19.6 m/s.`, selects the first mark point, presses ctrl+b, and resumes from Home.
- **Expected:** The pending self-mark step and its selected point remain available after a voluntary leave/resume, or the app warns that the current marking choices will be discarded.
- **Actual:** The pilot prints `after_submit TickPanel`, `selected_before_leave ['1']`, `after_leave_screen HomeScreen`, `resumed_panel LongPanel`, `resumed_text ''`, `working_in_lesson_log True`, `answer_events 0`. Ctrl+b leaves without warning because the TickPanel has no TextArea. Resume recreates a blank LongPanel and loses the selected marks; the student must retrieve and re-enter their working to get back to self-marking.
- **Impact:** A learner who pauses mid-marking loses their marking progress and is returned to the start of the question. Their typed working survives in the lesson log (B-015 fix), so this is a workflow/state restoration issue rather than permanent loss of that text.

### B-121 · P1 · screens.py · Leaving short-answer self-marking loses the submitted answer entirely
- **Found:** 18:34 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `app/tutor_app/screens.py` · `SessionScreen.submit`/`judge_short` keeps the submitted text in worker-local `data` and screen-local `pending_short` until `long_marks`; `action_leave` only checks `#panel TextArea`; resume constructs a fresh `ValuePanel`
- **Repro:** Make and scrub `/tmp/bugwatch-reports-crosscheck-round5` using `bash "$R/bugwatch/sandbox.sh" /tmp/bugwatch-reports-crosscheck-round5`, removing `source_path` from all scratch `.tutor/packs/*/plan.json`. With repo `PYTHONPATH`, `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, scratch `STEM_TUTOR_VAULT`, and `TERM_PROGRAM` unset, run `/tmp/bugwatch-scratch/reports-crosscheck-round5/short_resume.py`. This isolated fake-Claude Textual pilot disables AI, opens a controlled normal two-point short question, submits `distance moved north`, selects a self-mark point, presses ctrl+b, and resumes from Home. Inbox, Downloads, Claude state, and Almanac push are isolated/stubbed.
- **Expected:** Pausing at the self-mark list preserves the submitted answer and marking choices on resume, or warns that leaving will discard them.
- **Actual:** Prints `pending_panel TickPanel pending_short True`, `selected_before_leave ['0']`, `after_leave HomeScreen`, `resumed_panel ValuePanel`, `resumed_text ''`, `answer_events 0`, `text_in_notes [...] False`, `text_in_now False`. No confirmation appears. Neither the answer text nor mark selection is saved anywhere before the screen is popped.
- **Impact:** In the ordinary AI-off self-mark flow, a learner who presses the advertised menu key after submitting a written answer loses that answer and cannot retrieve it from the promised lesson transcript or Now. This is distinct from B-120: long-answer working survives in Lessons, while the short-answer text here has no saved copy.

### B-122 · P2 · publish.py · A failed asset copy exposes new notes while the old pack version stays active
- **Found:** 2026-10-20 18:37 HKT · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high for the failure ordering; occurrence frequency unmeasured
- **Where:** `build/publish.py` · `publish` renames the fully built `vN` pack, then copies notes/assets into the visible vault before writing `.tutor/packs/CURRENT`.
- **Repro:** With R set to the repo root, run `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH="$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" "$R/.venv/bin/python" /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/lesson-navigation-round5/publish_partial.py`. It constructs only a synthetic build and vault under `/tmp/bugwatch-scratch/lesson-navigation-round5/publish-partial`, publishes v1, changes a source note to v2, and injects an `OSError` on copying one synthetic SVG asset during the next publish. It never targets the real vault or repository build output.
- **Expected:** If publication aborts before activating v2, the visible note and the active v1 pack remain a consistent prior release, or the publisher reports a recoverable staging state without exposing unmatched content.
- **Actual:** The probe prints `before fault v1 'v1 note'`, then `simulated unreadable asset`, then `after fault v1 'v2 note only; matching pack not activated'`. `CURRENT` remains v1, while the visible note is already overwritten with v2 text; the unactivated v2 pack directory also exists.
- **Impact:** A missing/unreadable asset or failed copy late in publication can leave Obsidian showing new lesson text while the running tutor still loads old packs. This is version skew even without a live-app cache or pruning, distinct from B-094. The demonstrated error was injected; no real publisher failure or learner exposure was observed.

### B-123 · P2 · shortcuts_screen.py · Auto-read never speaks shortcut lesson explanations
- **Found:** 18:37 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `app/tutor_app/shortcuts_screen.py` · `_CourseScreen.card` calls `self.readable_now(text.plain)` without `auto=True`; `app/tutor_app/screens.py` · `ReadAloud.readable_now`; Settings switch label is "Read explanations, feedback and AI replies by themselves". `build/out/notes/How It Works.md` section 2 says "Turn on auto-read and pick a voice in Settings" under "A voice that reads to you," without a Shortcuts exception.
- **Repro:** Use a scrubbed `bash "$R/bugwatch/sandbox.sh" /tmp/bugwatch-notes-qol-round3` vault (remove `source_path` from every scratch `.tutor/packs/*/plan.json`). With `R` defined as the repo root, run `env -u TERM_PROGRAM PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH="$R/app:$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" "$R/.venv/bin/python" /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/notes-qol-round3/auto_shortcuts_probe.py`. The pilot uses fake Claude, stubs Almanac push, isolates external paths and replaces the speaker with a recording fake; it enables `tts_enabled=True`, `tts_auto=True`, enters the published Shortcuts course and reveals two lesson parts.
- **Expected:** With auto-read on, each displayed explanation part is sent to the speaker, as the Settings label promises.
- **Actual:** The first lesson part outputs `auto True calls [('stop', None)]`; after revealing the second part calls are `[('stop', None), ('stop', None)]`. No `say` call occurs. Every Shortcuts card uses `auto=False`, so manual ctrl+e is the only reading path.
- **Impact:** A learner using automatic narration receives silence through the Shortcuts lessons, despite enabling automatic explanation reading. This is separate from B-107/B-108: those omit information from speech; here playback is never requested.
- **Suggested fix:** Pass `auto=True` for the teaching/reveal/feedback cards intended to read themselves, while keeping questions manual as in the main lesson.

- **Actual:** The publisher returns `held: ['9702-1.1']`, `packs: 0`, `files_copied: 1`; the unreviewed note exists in the vault while no corresponding pack is active. With no pack file to read, `held_notes` is empty and the note filter cannot identify the held note.

### B-126 · P2 · speech.py · Plural electrical abbreviations are not expanded for read aloud
- **Found:** 18:46 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/speech.py` · `_ABBREV` only matches singular `p.d.` and `e.m.f.`; `app/tutor_app/tts.py` · `spoken`
- **Repro:** With `R` set to the repo root, run `env PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH="$R/app:$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" "$R/.venv/bin/python" -c 'from tutor_app.tts import spoken; print(spoken("Compare the p.d.s across two equal series resistors.")); print(spoken("The p.d. across each resistor is equal."))'`. The first text is published item `9702-10.3-i05` (`build/out/packs/9702/9702-10.3.json`); plural `p.d.s` also occurs in `9702-10.2` teaching and `9702-10.3` feedback. Published `9702-10.1.4` teaching starts `E.m.f. is ... P.d. across ...` and has the same capitalization edge case. Pure conversion only; no audio engine or player was run.
- **Expected:** Electrical abbreviations in stem and teaching text become unambiguous spoken terms, e.g. “potential differences” and “electromotive force,” including plural and sentence-initial forms.
- **Actual:** `spoken` gives `Compare the p. d. s across two equal series resistors.`; singular `p.d.` becomes `potential difference`. The teaching card yields `E. m. f. is energy supplied ... P. d. across a component ...`, whereas later lowercase `p.d.` expands correctly. The exact audible rendering of the unexpanded letters was not measured.
- **Impact:** Read aloud presents a core electricity question and teaching card as punctuation-separated letters instead of the concepts being taught, unlike otherwise identical singular/lowercase uses. This is separate from B-116's ambiguous option/point A label.
- **Suggested fix:** Expand plural and case-variant electrical abbreviations before punctuation tokenization; keep the singular conversion.

### B-127 · P2 · tts.py · Multipart speech pruning can evict a component while it is playing
- **Found:** 18:48 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `app/tutor_app/tts.py` · `Speaker._audio` stores `paths.append(path)` then calls `join_wavs(paths, joined)`; `Speaker._one` calls `self._prune()` after every new synthesis; `_prune` removes the oldest files without excluding paths used by the current reading. `screens.py` · `spoken_feedback` builds multipart readings beginning with a repeated verdict such as `Correct.`
- **Repro:** Set R to the repo; run `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH="$R/app:$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" "$R/.venv/bin/python" /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/coordinator/tts_prune_inflight.py`. The script creates 200 tiny valid WAVs only beneath /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/coordinator, with cached `Correct.` oldest; uses the production LIVE_KEEP=200; replaces synthesize with a tiny-file writer; calls `_audio(['Correct.', 'A newly requested explanation.'], None)`. No Kokoro, player, real vault, or AI is used.
- **Expected:** Both components remain available until the requested reading has been joined.
- **Actual:** The new explanation makes the cache reach 201 files; pruning deletes the cached first component. `join_wavs` raises FileNotFoundError. `_run` catches that failure, sets path=None and reports Couldn't read that aloud (FileNotFoundError), so no part plays.
- **Impact:** Once the live cache fills, a multipart feedback reading can fail completely when an older reused component is pruned during preparation. This is the concrete functional failure related to Q-012's cache policy, beyond its previously demonstrated unnecessary regeneration. Reproduction proves ordering at the real cap; normal-use frequency was not measured.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep every component used by an in-flight reading until joining finishes; prune afterwards while protecting the completed output.
- 18:48 SWEEP · coordinator TTS cache lifetime · fake WAV writer at production 200-file cap reproduced cached first component eviction during multipart preparation; no engine/player/real vault · finding: multipart reading FileNotFoundError, related Q-012

- **Found:** 18:50 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/speech.py` · `_RE_TABLE_ROW` / `speakable`; `app/tutor_app/tts.py` · `spoken`
- **Repro:** With `R` set to the repo root, run `env PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH="$R/app:$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" "$R/.venv/bin/python" -c 'import json; from pathlib import Path; from tutor_app.tts import spoken; d=json.loads((Path("build/out/packs/S1/S1-2.json")).read_text()); print(next((x["id"],spoken(x["stem"])) for x in d["items"] if x["id"]=="S1-2-i13"))'` from the repo. Published active items `S1-2-i13`, `S1-2-i15`, and `S1-2-r03` have Markdown frequency tables in their stems and no `speech` override. This is pure text conversion; no synthesis/player ran.
- **Expected:** Narration includes each class interval and its frequency, including comparisons such as `10≤t<20`, so the learner has the data for quartile/percentile interpolation.
- **Actual:** For `S1-2-i13`, the visible stem has intervals `10≤t<20`, `20≤t<25`, `25≤t<40`, `40≤t<60` and frequencies `8, 12, 12, 8`; `spoken` outputs `The table shows the delivery times, t minutes, for forty parcels. Use linear interpolation to estimate the upper quartile, Q three.` All intervals and frequencies disappear. The same happens for the other two IDs. The converter documents its table-dropping behavior, but these published items provide no spoken replacement.
- **Impact:** A learner relying on read aloud cannot calculate the requested quartile, median, interquartile range, or percentiles because the essential data and interval boundaries are missing. Different cause from B-107's omitted MCQ choices and B-108's unavailable diagram description.
- **Suggested fix:** Give table-backed published items a spoken row-by-row description, or have read aloud speak the table cells and headers in order.

### B-131 · P0 · grade.py · Correct unrounded science calculations lose marks contrary to Cambridge calculation guidance
- **Found:** 18:53 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/grade.py` · `_figures_count` · `return sf > max(allowed) and not maths`; `_grade_numeric` returns score 0.5/NOTATION for that result. Active published items `9701-2.1-i13` and `9702-10.2-i25`. `tests/test_grade.py::test_figures_still_count_where_they_carry_meaning` explicitly expects an unrounded science value to lose credit; `build/out/notes/How It Works.md` repeats this rule in the Number answers section.
- **Repro:** Define R as repo; run `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 R="$R" PYTHONPATH="$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" "$R/.venv/bin/python" /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/coordinator/cambridge_precision_probe.py`. Chemistry i13 asks for relative formula mass of CaCO3 from 40.1, 12.0, 16.0, so exact arithmetic is 40.1+12.0+3*16.0=100.1. Physics i25 seed0 asks for current through 3Ω+4Ω from 18V, so I=18/7 A. Neither stem specifies significant figures or asks for an instrument reading. Probe uses pure grading only.
- **Expected:** Credit numerically correct calculations at additional precision when no precision is specified, subject to explicit question/mark-scheme exceptions. Official Cambridge 9701/21 May/June2024 p5 section6 and 9702/21 May/June2024 p4 section6 say examiners should compare correct answers after rounding to mark-scheme precision when the question gives no required significant figures; measured values may be exceptions. Sources read online: https://www.cambridgeinternational.org/Images/567189-june-2024-mark-scheme-paper-21.pdf and https://www.cambridgeinternational.org/Images/567863-june-2024-mark-scheme-paper-21.pdf . This does not authorize arbitrary rounding, insufficient precision, missing units, or ignoring explicit practical/measurement requirements.
- **Actual:** Chemistry `100.1` gets correct=True but score=0.5, error=NOTATION, detail=`4 s.f. (want 2/3)`; `100` gets score1. Physics calculator value `2.571428571 A` gets score0.5 with `10 s.f. (want 2/3)`; `2.57 A` gets score1. Both unrounded answers round to the accepted answers, and the chemistry answer exactly matches the stored target.
- **Impact:** Correct science calculations are treated as lost-mark answers and recorded below full credit even though the question does not impose that precision. The learner specifically requested alignment with real 9701/9702 exam rules. This is a documented existing policy/test mismatch with the external exam guidance, not a regression of the earlier fewer-figures fix in commit9f2dba6. B-057/B-099 concern explicit precision instructions and do not cover this case.
- **Suggested fix:** Align automatic marking, regression expectations and learner guide with the Cambridge calculation rule; retain genuine explicit-precision and measurement exceptions rather than treating every sf_ok cap as compulsory.
- 18:54 SWEEP · learner-requested Cambridge significant-figure rules · read official9701/21 and9702/21 June2024 calculation guidance; independently reproduced chemistry100.1 and physics18/7 calculator-value penalties with rounded controls; checked existing tests/guide/FIXES distinction · finding: unrounded science calculation wrong-credit policy
- 18:54 REPLY B-131 · coordinator independently verified finite exact physics example9702-5.2-i09 seed0: 3kg raised2.5m with g9.81 gives exactly73.575J; no stated precision/instrument reading. 73.575 J scores0.5 NOTATION while73.6 J scores1.0. This strengthens the physics case beyond the nonterminating18/7 calculator approximation. Hunter scratch script /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/notes-qol-round3/unrounded_sigfig_repro.py includes all three examples.

### B-135 · P1 · panels.py · Rapid repeated Enter answers the next unseen MCQ as A with confidence 1
- **Found:** 19:00 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `app/tutor_app/panels.py` · `ContinuePanel.action_go`, `ChoicePanel._picked`, `ChoicePanel._conf`; `app/tutor_app/screens.py` · `SessionScreen.panel_done` and `advance`
- **Repro:** Run `bash "$R/bugwatch/sandbox.sh" /tmp/bugwatch-lesson-navigation-round5`, remove `source_path` from every scratch `.tutor/packs/*/plan.json`, and define R as this repo. From the repo, run `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH="$R/app:$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" env -u TERM_PROGRAM "$R/.venv/bin/python" /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/lesson-navigation-round5/rapid_enter.py`. The fake-Claude Textual pilot at 80x24 isolates inbox/Downloads/Claude state, stubs Almanac push, opens Test prep for published `9701-2.1`, answers Q1 intentionally with `a`, `1`, then sends `enter`, `enter`, `enter` without pauses from Q1's Next panel. Q2 was still queued before this key burst.
- **Expected:** Repeating the Next key should not record a response to the newly displayed question before the learner chooses an option and confidence.
- **Actual:** The first Enter advances to Q2; the next Enter selects the default highlighted option A; the third selects default confidence 1 and marks Q2. Fresh-vault event log: Q1 `9701-2.1-i04` response A/confidence 1, then Q2 `9701-2.1-r02` response A/confidence 1, graded wrong with `CONCEPT`/`m5`. The pilot made no deliberate Q2 selection; it is already at Q2's feedback/Next panel.
- **Impact:** A brief repeated-key burst after Next can silently record an unseen question as the learner's wrong answer, altering their score, misconception history, and repair plan.
- **Suggested fix:** Prevent key events that triggered Next from also acting on the newly mounted question/confidence panels; require a fresh deliberate selection after the transition.

- **Suggested fix:** Treat Home.md refresh failure as a note-sync error after preserving the real `end()` summary, allowing `SessionScreen.finish` to show it.

### B-141 · P0 · S1-3.json · Exact flag rejects allowed rounded probability answers
- **Found:** 19:14 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `build/out/packs/S1/S1-3.json` · active numeric `S1-3-i02`, `S1-3-i21`, `S1-3-i22`; `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/grade.py` · `_grade_numeric` sets `rounds = not ans.get("exact")` despite `sf_ok: [2,3]`.
- **Repro:** With `R` the repo root, run `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH="$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" "$R/.venv/bin/python" /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/notes-qol-round3/exact_rounded_scan.py` (pure pack reads/grade calls). Directly, `grade.grade_item(S1-3-i02, {'kind':'value','value':'1/3'})` scores 1.0, but correctly rounded `0.33` and `0.333` score 0 and request judgement. `S1-3-i21` has target 2/7≈0.285714: correct `0.29` and `0.286` score 0. `S1-3-i22` has target 4/7≈0.571429: correct `0.57` and `0.571` score 0. The stems only ask to calculate probabilities; none says to give an exact fraction.
- **Expected:** An answer rounded to the published allowed `[2,3]` significant figures receives credit, or the question explicitly requires an exact fraction and its metadata reflects that.
- **Actual:** `exact: true` forces a 1e-9 value comparison and disables the rounding path, so only a literal fraction or long calculator decimal passes. The correctly rounded 2/3-figure values are rejected even though `sf_ok` lists those counts.
- **Impact:** Learners using ordinary calculator rounding lose marks and cannot advance automatically on these valid answers. This is distinct from B-131's excess-precision science answers and B-140's wrongly accepted near values.

### B-142 · P2 · store.py · Interrupted in-place fallback truncates learner-owned note text
- **Found:** 19:20 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high for the conditional failure, low for its real-world frequency
- **Where:** `plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts/tutorlib/store.py` · `write_text` · `except OSError: path.write_text(text, encoding="utf-8")` after `os.replace(tmp, path)` fails; `tutorlib/views.py` · `_write` uses this for My Notes
- **Repro:** Define R as repo, then run `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH="$R/plugin/stem-tutor/skills/tutor/scripts" "$R/.venv/bin/python" /tmp/bugwatch-scratch/reports-crosscheck-round5/inplace_fallback_probe.py`. It uses only a temporary `My Notes/scratch.md`, containing a managed status block and a learner-owned `## My working` paragraph. It forces `os.replace` to raise (the documented fallback condition), then makes the destination's in-place `Path.write_text` write a short prefix and raise `OSError`, simulating an interruption or disk-full during that fallback.
- **Expected:** If the fallback write cannot finish, the previous readable note, including the learner's paragraph, stays intact or is recovered from the complete temporary copy.
- **Actual:** The destination is truncated to `'# Scratch note\n\n<!-- stem-tutor:status -->\n'`; `target_has_owned_text False`. The hidden `.scratch.md.*.tmp` still contains the full updated text, but normal note reads use the damaged destination and there is no automatic temp recovery. `write_text` propagates the second error.
- **Impact:** On a mount that refuses atomic replacement while permitting in-place writes, a later write interruption can remove learner-owned text from the visible My Notes file. The complete hidden temp may allow manual recovery; this probe does not establish how often either filesystem failure occurs. This differs from B-092's concurrent editor overwrite, and from B-132/B-139's separate session/note ordering gaps.
- **Suggested fix:** Preserve the old destination before attempting the non-atomic fallback, or restore from the complete temporary file if that fallback fails; keep the note's learner text recoverable.

### B-149 · P2 · store.py · A non-object JSON line prevents all event history from loading
- **Found:** 2026-11-01 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `src/tutorlib/store.py` · `Vault._read_file` calls `e.get("id")` after `json.loads`, without checking that the decoded value is an event object. `Tutor.__init__` calls `last_event_id` during startup.
- **Repro:** From the repo root, run `PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python - <<'PY'` followed by `import json,tempfile; from pathlib import Path; from tutorlib.store import Vault; d=tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(); v=Vault(Path(d.name)); p=v.tutor/'events'/'2026-11.jsonl'; p.parent.mkdir(parents=True); p.write_text(json.dumps({'id':'a','ts':'2026-11-01T10:00:00+08:00','type':'gaps','add':['a']})+'\nnull\n'+json.dumps({'id':'b','ts':'2026-11-01T10:01:00+08:00','type':'gaps','add':['b']})+'\n'); print(list(v.events()))` then `PY`. The temporary vault contains only synthetic events.
- **Expected:** Report the non-object line as bad and read the intact events around it, preserving original file bytes, as for malformed JSON lines.
- **Actual:** Both `list(v.events())` and `v.last_event_id()` raise `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'get'`; neither intact event is returned. JSON arrays, strings, numbers and booleans have the same issue.
- **Impact:** A syntactically valid non-object record introduced by a damaged or manually edited sync copy can block startup and prevent replay of otherwise intact learner history. This is an edge case; ordinary engine-written events are objects.
- **Suggested fix:** Classify decoded non-dictionaries as bad lines before event access, preserve their bytes, and exercise replay and startup with intact records on both sides.
- 2026-11-01 17:17 SWEEP · baseline · full suite stopped after 2% and 10,344 collected items; estimated runtime offered poor feedback, switching to deterministic focused suites

### B-151 · P0 · grade.py · Small nonzero quantities accept answers hundreds of thousands of times too large
- **Found:** 2026-11-01 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `src/tutorlib/grade.py` · `_close` uses the absolute floor `max(tol_rel * abs(b), 1e-12)` for every target; `src/tutorlib/challenge_gen.py` · `to_item` admits finite small numeric answers and `agrees` uses the same grader to check an independent solution.
- **Repro:** From the repo root run `PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python /tmp/bugwatch-engine-tiny.py`. The disposable script constructs a valid typed Challenge question converting 1.00 eV to 1.60e-19 J, using the production converter and grading paths; it makes no provider call or vault write.
- **Expected:** The correct `1.60e-19 J` passes; `1.00e-13 J`, which is 625000 times larger, and `0 J` fail, including the blind-solve agreement gate.
- **Actual:** `1.60e-19 J` and `1.00e-13 J` both receive full marks and `agrees=True`; `0 J` receives half marks and `agrees=True`. An answer `1.00e-11 J`, outside the fixed absolute floor, fails. This is distinct from B-140's former one-percent relative tolerance.
- **Impact:** Legitimate Challenge questions involving energy, charge or other small SI quantities can award marks for grossly wrong answers; the same defect can falsely accept a mismatched independent solver result. This is demonstrated with a synthetic valid question, not claimed for any currently published item.
- **Suggested fix:** Use a scale-sensitive comparison with a deliberate policy for zero; test near-zero quantities, normal magnitudes, exact answers and the Challenge agreement gate.

### B-154 · P2 · screens.py · Empty search focus stops Escape from leaving topic folders
- **Found:** current run · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `app/tutor_app/screens.py` · `PickerScreen.action_back` calls `action_up`; `action_up` returns when `isinstance(self.focused, Input)`.
- **Repro:** Run `bash bugwatch/check.sh tests/app/test_picker_empty_search.py -q`. It uses the existing disposable fixture vault and synthetic multi-course picker metadata. Open Learn, enter Chemistry, press `/` to focus Search without typing, then press Esc.
- **Expected:** Esc returns to the subject folders, with the topic list focused, consistent with the visible "Esc to go back" instruction.
- **Actual:** Picker path remains `['chem']` and Search stays focused. Further Esc presses have the same result. The empty search is not cleared by the first branch, while the folder-up branch rejects Input focus.
- **Impact:** Keyboard learners cannot use Esc to back out of a topic folder after opening an empty search; they need to discover Down or Tab to move focus first.
- **Suggested fix:** Return focus to the option list before the explicit Back action goes up; preserve Left/Backspace editing behavior in Search.

### B-155 · P2 · timeout.py · Resistant worker children survive the course timeout
- **Found:** 2026-11-01 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `src/tutor_workers/timeout.py` · `run` after `TimeoutExpired`.
- **Repro:** Run the production timeout wrapper with a disposable Python parent that spawns a child ignoring SIGTERM and then sleeps. With a 0.5-second deadline, run returns 124 but ps still reports the child sleeping. Probe cleans the child with SIGKILL; no model is launched.
- **Expected:** Timeout cleanup terminates the whole worker process group, including children that resist SIGTERM.
- **Actual:** SIGTERM kills the parent; its wait returns immediately, so the SIGKILL branch is skipped and the resistant child keeps running.
- **Impact:** A child worker may continue computing or consuming provider allowance after the course deadline even though the wrapper reports a timeout.
