### B-171 · P1 · model.py · Incorrect answers do not break the misconception recovery streak
- **Found:** 2026-11-01 · **Method:** actual Tutor.answer regression · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** `src/tutorlib/model.py` · `_apply_answer` misconception `mis_streak` update; `insights._misconceptions` promises two right answers in a row.
- **Repro:** `bash bugwatch/check.sh tests/test_misconception_streak.py -q`. In a disposable fixture vault, present the existing vector MCQ without shuffle, answer A (diagnosed m1), B (right), C (wrong without a misconception) or ? (do not know), then B (right). All three new regressions fail before the patch, including old-cache reload.
- **Expected:** The intervening incorrect answer breaks the consecutive independent-success streak. m1 remains active with streak 1, and default_method still chooses refutation until another consecutive right answer.
- **Actual:** `_apply_answer` leaves the prior streak unchanged on undiagnosed failures, so the final B clears m1 and the teaching policy stops refutation. Reloading model 1.1.8 cache retains the incorrect result.
- **Impact:** A learner can lose targeted misconception teaching despite failing an intervening check; deterministic replay currently reproduces the same wrong inference. Distinct from B-007: these are unassisted wrong answers between independent successes, not hinted answers being counted as successes.

### B-178 · P2 · experiments.py · Exact ten-point experimental gains can be rejected by binary rounding
- **Found:** 2026-11-01 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high

The result reports mean_diff=0.1, p_first_better=1.0, but decision=no_difference. Equal-sized 0.8/0.7 gains instead select worked_faded. Reversing the first example similarly rejects the second arm.
- **Expected:** A completed sufficiently certain experimental gain of exactly ten percentage points satisfies the documented >=10-point adoption threshold in either direction, independent of the baseline score.
- **Actual:** Binary subtraction produces a mean just below 0.10 for some baseline scores; direct comparison rejects the qualifying gain while the result displays it as 0.1.
- **Impact:** Equivalent valid retest evidence can adopt a winning teaching method or discard it solely because of floating-point representation, leaving policy on the default method despite reaching the stated threshold. No learner events or production resources were modified by the probe.

### B-180 · P2 · blurt.py · Ordinary final-e plurals are marked missed and pulled into Review
- **Found:** 2026-11-01 · **Method:** ran · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** src/tutorlib/blurt.py · _stem strips the whole es suffix from distances/forces/charges, producing distanc/forc/charg rather than distance/force/charge.
- **Repro:** Run `bash bugwatch/check.sh tests/test_blurt_inflections.py -q`. The equivalent plural answer `Distances are scalar; displacements have directions.` fails with recalled=[], because distances loses its final e and only displacement matches. One singular control passes and one plural regression fails.
- **Expected:** Regular singular/plural key-term variants match consistently, so this recalled idea keeps its existing due date and answer marks remain unchanged; the original blurt text stays in the lesson record.
- **Actual:** Blurt classifies the plural answer as missed, and its normal event fold pulls the studied KC's review due date forward despite the equivalent singular answer counting as recalled.
- **Impact:** Ordinary learner wording changes recall classification and review scheduling. The same suffix bug affects common STEM terms forces and charges. This is a concrete inflection-matching defect, not a claim that keyword matching can grade free text semantically.

### B-185 · P2 · packs.py · Stepped template sampling can exceed its declared maximum
- **Found:** 2026-11-01 · **Method:** public packs.instantiate and ordinary pack validator · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** src/tutorlib/packs.py · _sample uses int(round((max-min)/step)) as the largest sample index.
- **Repro:** Run `bash bugwatch/check.sh tests/test_template_sampling.py -q`. A validator-accepted numeric template declares x with min=1, max=9, step=3 and answer=x. Across deterministic seeds 0–19, public instantiate returns {1,4,7,10}; the regression fails because 10 is outside the declared maximum. The valid inclusive grid is {1,4,7}; max need not itself be a grid point.
- **Expected:** Every stepped sample is min plus an integral number of steps within inclusive min/max bounds; ordinary valid grids retain their existing precision and RNG behavior.
- **Actual:** Rounding the number of steps up authorizes one extra sample beyond max for nondivisible intervals.
- **Impact:** Accepted templates can present values outside their authored domain and compute keys from those values. No active published occurrence is claimed; the source item is unchanged and no production/resource/provider work occurs.

### B-187 · P1 · session.py · Normal multi-KC batches present and count the same fixed source twice
- **Found:** 2026-11-01 · **Method:** actual public Tutor.start/next/answer · **Confidence:** high
- **Where:** src/tutorlib/session.py · _step_review/_step_practice, _step_exit and _step_sweep select both KC items before _questions records either as used/open; _pick therefore cannot see the first staged selection.

A disposable two-KC pool contains either one shared fixed item or separate fixed item IDs with the same canonical source. Public Test produces sweep; public Review with due KCs produces review, with non-due introduced KCs produces practice, then exit. Both batch and one-at-a-time modes stage the same source twice; answering the current batch writes two answer events. All 16 normal-flow regressions fail with duplicate identities and two new answers where one independent fixed exposure is expected.
- **Expected:** A normal question batch excludes an already staged fixed source or its canonical aliases, while independently assessed source parts and independently sampled template variants remain eligible.
- **Actual:** The same fixed source is presented twice in one batch or shown again from the queue immediately after its first answer; both answers enter the learner evidence. Exact shared IDs also overwrite sweep's per-item target map while staging.
- **Impact:** A repeated question can inflate progress/spacing evidence after its answer has already been exposed, and sweep target attribution can move to the later KC. This is separate from fixed diagnostic-only B181/B182; all four ordinary phase paths are reached through public session APIs. No source edits, production/resource/provider calls or historical event rewrites occurred.

### B-192 · P2 · prompts.py + session.py · Images-only questions lose the first-page Ask attachment after marking
- **Found:** Validator-accepted source schema followed through ordinary public Tutor start/next/answer and saved-session restart. **Method:** ran. **Confidence:** high for the supported-shape regression; no active occurrence claimed.
- **Where:** src/tutorlib/session.py · _mark saves recent.image from inst.get("image") but omits inst.images; app/tutor_app/prompts.py · focus returns that recent cache before reading its exact answer event; focus_image therefore returns None although event.question retains images[].
- **Repro:** From stem-tutor run `PYTHONPATH="$PWD:$PWD/tests:$PWD/tests/app:$PWD/build" bash bugwatch/check.sh -p bugwatch.probe_isolation /tmp/bugwatch-images-only-recent/test_recent_attachment.py -q --tb=short`. The source fixture is authored with images[] and no optional image before Tutor loads it; build.validate_pack.validate returns [], and publish._validate_pack_assets accepts its two Library-resolved images. No private recent mutation or provider request occurs.
- **Expected:** The existing single-image Ask consumer retains the same first source image when following up on the just-marked question. It need not receive all source pages, and immutable source/event bytes must remain unchanged.
- **Actual:** Public presentation sets view.image to images[0], and prompts.focus_image returns its verified Path while the question is open. After a correctly marked answer, session.recent contains image=None and no images[], so focus_image returns None. The exact answer event still contains the two ordered image references. Both immediate and public restart controls fail on `focused == first`, with source and event bytes unchanged. Focused gate: 2 failed in 0.38 seconds. The disposable source-pack SHA-256 is 13ae4bdefd99fd176c2a2958d4fba939d693bede03dc8d105a1109f9d19534ad; the proof uses registered input media via tests/library_assets.py.
- **Impact:** An otherwise supported source shape loses its already-supported diagram attachment for a just-marked follow-up, weakening grounded explanation. This is a separate images-array cache-loss cause from B-075's former menu reconstruction issue. Independent read-only v274 inventory found zero active images-only records: all 57 nonempty topical images arrays also declare a primary equal to images[0]; 102 packs and 1,532 topical items were scanned. Thus current shipped occurrence is not asserted, and structural validator acceptance is not teaching approval.
- **Suggested fix:** Recover only missing attachment fields from the exact focused recent event into a prompt-local copy; preserve current open-focus precedence, cached/recorded answer content and immutable history. Do not guess from latest/unrelated events or expand multi-image provider capability.

### B-193 · P2 · reliability.py · Malformed candidate structure crashes retry admission instead of pausing safely
- **Found:** Codex-only watch, deterministic regression; method ran, confidence high.
- **Where:** src/tutor_workers/reliability.py, launch_problem, diagram preflight uses pack.get and teach.values without validating object shapes.
- **Repro:** bash bugwatch/check.sh tests/test_workflow_reliability.py::test_malformed_candidate_shape_pauses_retry_instead_of_crashing -q --tb=short
- **Expected:** An invalid candidate produces a preflight reason before catalog lookups or another paid retry; failure state and candidate bytes remain unchanged.
- **Actual:** A JSON list at candidate root raises AttributeError: 'list' object has no attribute 'get'. A nonempty list or string in teach similarly reaches an unsupported values method.
- **Impact:** A malformed worker candidate breaks workflow admission reporting rather than preserving a clear paused diagnostic. No active teaching-content occurrence is asserted.
- **Suggested fix:** Validate candidate and teaching-card containers at the existing diagram preflight boundary; return the existing preflight diagnostic on invalid shapes.
- 2026-11-01 22:01 REPLY B-193 · Ranked hypotheses: missing candidate container validation (actual trace at pack.get); teaching-card container mismatch (same unchecked boundary); malformed JSON parsing (disproved: JSON loads successfully). Regression fails in 0.15 seconds before asset lookup. Narrow shape validation next.

### B-194 · P1 · screens.py · Completed guarded AI replies remain below the session viewport
- **Found:** Codex parallel watch; deterministic repro, confidence high; method ran.
- **Where:** SessionScreen._stream updates the final AI card without scrolling after the card grows. ChatScreen already scrolls after its final update.
- **Repro:** bash bugwatch/check.sh tests/app/test_session_reply_visibility.py -q --tb=short
- **Expected:** The latest completed hint, refusal or login-error card is visible after sending from the session.
- **Actual:** Nine public UI controls at 60x24, 80x24 and 140x46 fail because the final card is outside the viewport. Native before capture /tmp/bugwatch-ui-current-20261101/native/session-hint-80x24.svg.png.
- **Impact:** Learners can miss the explanation or provider failure and think the app has not responded.

### B-195 · P2 · extraction contract · Inclusive source page range is misread as two selected pages
- **Found:** Codex parallel watch; deterministic repro, confidence high; method ran.
- **Where:** extraction/extract.py worker prompt emits selected_pages [start,end] without an explicit inclusive interval contract.
- **Repro:** Inspect Work/Workers/extraction/allsource-20261101-9701-notes-first/extract.log and preserved candidate; the worker reports inspecting selected pages 1 and 4.
- **Expected:** Every page in the requested inclusive range is inspected before judging extraction coverage.
- **Actual:** The first four-page chemistry notes job inspects only pages 1 and 4 and returns empty arrays; text on pages 2 and 3 contains isotope figures.
- **Impact:** Extraction silently misses requested pages and relevant diagrams.

### B-196 · P2 · extraction worker · Reader cache writes outside extraction worker writable directories
- **Found:** Codex parallel watch; deterministic repro, confidence high; method ran.
- **Where:** extraction/extract.py _worker starts source readers without a job-local STEM_LIBRARY_CACHE.
- **Repro:** Inspect Work/Workers/extraction/allsource-20261101-9701-notes-first/extract.log lines 7677-7692 and run the new worker-cache regression in tests/test_extraction.py.
- **Expected:** Prescribed source rendering and cropping can write their disposable cache inside the job directory.
- **Actual:** The worker log records reader/render cache permission failure; worker manually redirects cache to continue.
- **Impact:** Extraction wastes requests and can fail before reading or cropping verified source regions.

### B-197 · P0 · grade.py · Tiny expression answers earn full credit for false identities
- **Found:** Fresh engine grader probe, method ran, confidence high.
- **Where:** src/tutorlib/grade.py, expression equality comparator _equal, sampled comparison uses max(1, absolute values).
- **Repro:** Grade expression key 0.000000000001*x against answer 0, twice the coefficient, or the coefficient times (x+1). All currently receive full credit in the agent's public grade_item repro; narrow regression added in engine lane.
- **Expected:** Nonzero expressions and changed coefficients/terms are rejected regardless of overall numeric scale; equivalent forms and floating-point cancellation identities remain accepted.
- **Actual:** The absolute 1e-9 floor masks every nonzero sampled difference of these tiny false expressions; constant 1e-12 also accepts zero.
- **Impact:** Learners earn full credit for mathematically incorrect expressions at small scales. This is distinct from numeric grading tolerance.
- **Suggested fix:** Use a bounded scale-aware equivalence comparison while preserving deliberate cancellation handling and expression timeout guards.
- 2026-11-01 22:03 REPLY B-197 · Root cause confirmed by preserved tiny parsed coefficients and nonzero sample differences masked by max(1, sample magnitudes). Engine agent writes tiny false/true and cancellation controls before repair.

### B-198 · P1 · policy.py + insights.py · Exported CAIE exam dates never reach scheduling consumers
- **Found:** Codex-only parallel audit; method ran, confidence high.
- **Where:** policy.exam_date and insights._exam_label match course identifiers against display short names instead of official paper codes.

### B-200 · P2 · local_ocr.py · OCR cancellation can crash when macOS reports permission failure for an already exited process
- **Found:** Full-suite cancellation failure, followed by a real rapid-exit process reproduction on this Mac; method ran, confidence high.
- **Where:** NativeOCR._stop, the posix process-group signal helper handles ProcessLookupError but not the observed macOS exiting-group EPERM race.
- **Repro:** tests/app/test_local_ocr.py::test_reset_cancels_active_and_queued_ocr_without_launching_the_old_queue failed in the full suite. The UI agent reproduces the race with rapid /usr/bin/true process exit and concurrent _stop calls; os.killpg raises PermissionError while returncode is None, then os.getpgid confirms the PID is already gone.
- **Expected:** Reset cancels active and queued OCR, reaps an already exited child and never launches the old queue. Genuine permission errors for a still-existing process remain visible.
- **Actual:** Reset raises PermissionError at local_ocr.py line50 during process cleanup, instead of finishing cancellation.
- **Impact:** A timing-dependent local OCR cancellation can interrupt app cleanup or reset. Forty isolated ordinary OCR repetitions pass; the rapid-exit harness exposes the process lifecycle race.
- **Suggested fix:** Handle the permission failure only after a fresh PID lookup proves that process has disappeared; preserve waiting/reaping and all live-process permission failures.
- 2026-11-01 22:16 REPLY B-200 · Root cause confirmed by real Mac rapid-exit process evidence, not inferred from a lone flaky assertion. Deterministic gone/live PID controls and existing queue-cancellation regressions will verify narrow repair.

### B-201 · P1 · extraction reliability · Failed extraction and review gates do not consume the two-failure retry allowance
- **Found:** Two fake-provider regressions reproduce bypass for extraction and review roles; method ran, confidence high.
- **Where:** extraction/extract.py collection and check paths, which count worker process failures but leave structurally failed candidates and pending review verdicts uncounted.
- **Repro:** The extraction agent runs two distinct incomplete candidate attempts and, separately, two pending reviewer attempts using a disposable fake provider. Both controls fail because a third launch is admitted instead of reporting paused after two failures.
- **Expected:** Each distinct unsuccessful paid attempt consumes one bounded failure allowance; repeated read-only check polling must not consume additional attempts. A third launch stops until explicit operator diagnosis/reset.
- **Actual:** All worker processes return exit0, so the reliability failure lists stay empty despite failed content/review gates, and a third paid stage is allowed.
- **Impact:** A repeated content or review blocker can spend on unproductive model retries beyond the documented limit.
- **Suggested fix:** Account for failed gates once per actual attempt, preserving immutable failure evidence and requiring explicit unstick after two failures. Do not charge stale reviews after controller changes or repeated checks.

### B-203 · P0 · Blurt feedback · Blurt exposes teaching answers while a skipped-question guard remains unresolved
- **Found:** Public start/next/answer/end/blurt sequence with source-authored disposable fixture; method ran, confidence high.
- **Where:** Engine Blurt feedback path creates teaching-card feedback without consulting the unresolved skipped-question guard, although normal notes/AI feedback must respect that guard.
- **Repro:** Start a lesson probe, skip the MCQ with ?, end or abandon the lesson, then run Blurt on the same topic. The fixture teaching note says Displacement is the vector quantity; the skipped protected MCQ asks for the vector quantity. Public marking_guards remains nonempty throughout.
- **Expected:** Skipped protected answers stay private until an attempt or explicit permitted retry resolves the guard. A refused feedback request preserves the learner’s draft and does not write answer-bearing views/logs.
- **Actual:** Blurt returns the teaching card containing the protected answer and writes it into a new Lessons log and Now.md while the original durable skipped guard remains unresolved.
- **Impact:** Learners can bypass the no-answer-before-attempt protection through an ordinary study mode transition.
- **Suggested fix:** Reuse the existing guard semantics before Blurt feedback is generated or written; keep normal unguarded Blurt behavior and user drafts intact.

### B-204 · P1 · Topic picker · Enter in an empty topic search submits a folder identifier instead of opening the folder

- **Impact:** Ordinary keyboard navigation can unexpectedly close the picker and submit an invalid topic identifier.
- **Suggested fix:** Reuse the existing folder activation route from search Enter while preserving valid leaf and multi-selection behavior.

### B-252 · P2 · codex_ai.py · Closing a completed Codex reply can raise a dead-process-group permission error
- **Found:** 2026-11-02 04:34 HKT · **Method:** full integration test traceback · **Confidence:** high for the observed cleanup failure
- **Where:** `app/tutor_app/codex_ai.py` · `CodexAI._stop`, reached from `_events` cleanup and `close -> reset`.
- **Repro:** Final frozen-source integration run, `/tmp/stem-bugwatch-20261102-suite-final.log`: `tests/app/test_codex_ai.py::test_failed_calls_return_actionable_errors_without_success_or_history[unreadable-error]` raises `PermissionError: [Errno 1] Operation not permitted` at `os.killpg(proc.pid, SIGTERM)` during `client.close()`. Exact node and preceding selections pass alone (1/50/246 tests), indicating an exit race; deterministic gone-versus-live group controls are being added in disposable fake-provider tests.
- **Expected:** A confirmed exited owned worker is reaped safely and repeated close/reset completes; genuine permission denial for a live process remains visible. No real provider calls are needed to test either case.
- **Actual:** `_stop` handles missing groups and timeouts but lets group EPERM escape. Initial stream cleanup retains the process after the exception; `close` retries it and raises instead of completing.
- **Impact:** A fast failed/completed Codex reply can turn screen/app cleanup into an exception on macOS. The failure prevented the expected actionable-error assertion from running; it was not an incorrect response-status result.
- **Suggested fix:** Handle group EPERM only after confirming the owned PID no longer exists, then reap it; retain live-denial propagation, descendant termination, cancellation and bounded waits. Reuse the proven OCR exit-race pattern where applicable.

### B-253 · P2 · codex_ai.py · A reply worker's resistant descendant survives cancellation after its parent exits
- **Found:** 2026-11-02 04:42 HKT · **Method:** real disposable owned process-group probe · **Confidence:** high for cleanup behavior, unmeasured for ordinary Codex children
- **Where:** `app/tutor_app/codex_ai.py` · `CodexAI._stop` group termination and early parent-return checks.
- **Repro:** `Work/Workers/bugwatch/20261101/codex-resistant-descendant-probe.json` records an owned new-session parent and a child that deliberately ignores SIGTERM. `_stop` terminates/reaps the parent (-15) and returns in 3.7ms, but the child's heartbeat advances from1 to5 over120ms and its group remains the owned parent PID. The probe always SIGKILLs only its exact owned test group in finally; no real Codex/provider call occurs.
- **Expected:** Cancelling an owned reply worker finishes its descendant group, even when the wrapper exits promptly on TERM; genuine signal denial remains visible and repeated cleanup does not target a reused unrelated group.
- **Actual:** A successful parent wait suppresses KILL escalation, so the resistant child remains alive. The already-reaped-parent entry route also needs bounded ownership review before changing early-return behavior.
- **Impact:** Cancellation can leave background work and resource use running after the app believes the reply worker was stopped. Actual paid-provider frequency or quota consumption is not inferred from this offline probe.
- **Suggested fix:** Finish the owned group after TERM and bounded parent wait, preserving child reaping, B252 dead-group handling, live-denial propagation and idempotency. Stage both live-parent and already-reaped-parent controls before selecting the narrowest safe implementation.

No learner data or shared timeout-controller changes; offline tests only.

- **Found:** 2026-11-02 · **Method:** learner traceback and deterministic disposable-vault reproduction · **Confidence:** high

Mount a real ValuePanel, remove it inside an app batch update, and deliver a MouseDown before the next compositor refresh. The real cached hit still returns the removed panel and content offset. Before the fix this reproduces the exact learner exception at Textual screen.py:1914: `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'region'`. No hit-test mocking or live model is used.
- **Expected:** A stale click during panel replacement does not crash, submit an answer or alter the current draft. Current widgets still accept clicks and text selection.
- **Actual:** Selection setup uses `content_widget.parent` after it becomes None, terminating the tutor.
- **Impact:** A normal mouse click timed during an answer-panel transition can exit the app; the traceback does not establish whether the latest learner draft was saved.
- **Suggested fix:** Before forwarding a new uncaptured MouseDown, discard a compositor target no longer attached to the app and request layout refresh. Keep normal, captured and already-forwarded events on their existing paths.
